世界経済：AI関連需要の広がりが世界景気をけん引。企業の景況感は堅調に推移
 製造業は節目50を上回る良好な水準を維持。非製造業は金融・消費関連を中心に加速
― 製造業はAI関連投資がけん引し、防衛支出の拡大も寄与。一方、中東情勢を受けた在庫積み増しの寄与は縮小傾向
― 非製造業は金融サービスやAI関連需要がけん引。エネルギー高で落ち込んでいた観光・娯楽も回復
 国・地域別では、米国では非製造業が大幅に改善。日本やユーロ圏では製造業を中心に改善

― 日本ではAI・半導体需要と外需拡大がけん引し、ユーロ圏は電気・電子部品を含む中間財の生産が好調。AI投資ブー
ムの広がりがうかがわれる
1
製造業の景況感（購買担当者景気指数） 非製造業の景況感（購買担当者景気指数）
（注） 日米欧：S&P Global、中：国家統計局。購買担当者景気指数：企業に対するアン
ケート調査結果から作成した指数。新規受注や生産高、雇用、出荷遅延などの指数
に一定のウエイトを掛けて算出。50が景気拡大・縮小の分岐点
（出所）S&P Global、中国国家統計局より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
（注） 日米欧：S&P Global、中：国家統計局。購買担当者景気指数（非製造業）：企業に対
するアンケート調査結果から作成した指数。50が景気拡大・縮小の分岐点
（出所）S&P Global、 中国国家統計局より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 3

金融市場概況：原油高を背景に日米金利が上昇
 日米株価は、9月下旬にハイテク株を中心に上昇
― 9月上旬は、日米株価とも軟調。原油高を背景とした日米金利上昇が重荷に
― 下旬に入ると、米AI関連株が買い戻されたことからナスダックが上昇に転じ、日経平均もつれ高の展開に
 ドル円は一時円高が進展したものの、日米金利差が拡大し、再度150円台後半まで円安が進む「往って来い」の展開に
― 日本・米国とも利上げを実施。先行きも利上げ継続見込みだが、米金利上昇幅がより大きく、日米金利差は拡大
― 日銀は物価上振れリスクに対応し、利上げペースを加速へ。次回利上げは26年12月会合と予想
2
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：個人消費は一時的に減速も、サービス中心に底堅さ維持
 7月実質個人消費支出は前月比＋0.0％と減速も、一時要因による下押しが大きく、消費の基調は崩れていないと評価
― 例年より前倒しで6月に実施された大手通販セールの反動から、財消費が減少
― 一方、そうした影響を受けづらいサービス消費については強さを維持。高齢化を背景に医療支出が拡大したほか、W杯
開催等を受け、娯楽サービス、外食・宿泊、輸送サービスなどの選択的支出も増加
 小売販売の高頻度データは、足元で伸びが再び高まっており、7月の減速が一時的であったことを示唆

3
（出所） 米商務省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：所得減速下でも株高による資産効果が消費を下支え
 消費が底堅い背景に、富裕層を中心とした株価上昇による資産効果の持続
― 株高を受けてインフレ下でも家計の実質金融資産は着実に増加。可処分所得対比でも高水準を維持。実質所得が弱含
む中でも、金融資産の増加が家計の購買力を下支え
 先行きは物価鈍化に伴う実質所得の改善も消費の追い風に
― 実質可処分所得は前年比＋0.5％と伸び悩みが継続。先行きは物価鈍化を受けて実質所得も伸び幅を徐々に持ち直す
見込み
4
（出所） 米商務省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 6

米国：雇用は予想外の大幅増。一時的要因の影響を受けつつも労働市場の底堅さを確認
 8月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋16.2万人と市場予想（同＋5.6万人）を大幅に上回る結果
― 過去2カ月も合わせて5.5万人の上方修正。速報段階で減少した7月もプラス転化
― 娯楽・宿泊（同＋6.2万人）や教育・医療（同＋2.8万人）を中心に民間就業者が伸びを高めたほか、政府部門も同＋3.5
万人増と押し上げに寄与
― 政府部門では、バックトゥスクールに伴い教員、スクールバス運転士、カフェテリアスタッフなどの雇用が増加（例年みら
れる季節的な動きであるが、統計上の調整にゆがみが生じている可能性）
5
（出所）米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（年初来変動幅、万人）
（年/月）
失業率は低位で横ばい
低失業率の一因に「少ない供給」
米国：失業率は低位安定。厳格化する移民政策が労働供給を一段と下押し
 失業率は4.1％と前月からほぼ横ばい。引き続きFOMC参加者が見込む長期均衡水準（Longer run）を下回る低水準
 低失業率の一因に労働参加率の低下。シニア層の退職増加に加え、移民政策の厳格化の影響も大
― 米移民・関税執行局（ICE）の摘発増など移民取り締まりが強化されるなか、メキシコ出身者などの労働人口は減少傾向
― 足元ではハイチなど複数国の一時保護資格（TPS、※）が相次ぎ終了。今後もエルサルバドルなどで有効期限を迎え、7

月以降の累計で最大100万人程度がTPSに基づく就労資格を失う可能性。労働供給を更に押し下げる要因に
（※）紛争や災害等により安全な帰国が困難な特定国の出身者に対し、一時的な在留・就労を認める制度
6
（出所）米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
失業率 移民の出身国別労働人口とTPS終了の対象国・規模
（注）TPS保有者数は各国について入手可能な最新値。ベネズエラは2023年指定分。9月以降のTPS終了
時期は現時点での有効期限であり、今後の延長・終了決定等により変更される可能性がある
（出所）米労働省、米移民評議会、米国土安全保障省、米議会調査局より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：エネルギー高・関税でディスインフレは足踏み、期待インフレも高止まり
 エネルギー高や関税が重石となり、インフレ率の低下は足踏み
―8月のCPIが前年比＋3.4％と横ばいにとどまるなか、コアCPIも同＋2.4％（7月同＋2.5％）と減速は緩やか。サービス価
格は原油高を背景に航空運賃などが上昇、財価格には関税の影響が残存
 消費者の期待インフレ率は3％台で高止まり、2％目標への収束はなお見通しづらい状況
―FRB高官からは、5年以上に及ぶ目標超過で高インフレが定着するリスクを警戒する声も
7

や原油価格上昇の影響が大きいと考えられる品目を分類

欧州：米国と比べて鈍い消費回復ペース。実質金融資産の目減りが消費回復の重石に
8
 原油価格高騰一服を受けて、家計のマインドは底打ち
―7～9月期以降は下押し圧力が緩和する公算も、米国に比べて消費回復ペースの鈍さは否めず
 最悪期は脱しつつある一方で、実質賃金の伸び悩みは継続。高インフレによる金融資産の目減りも逆風に
―4～6月期のユーロ圏妥結賃金は前年比＋2.4％と前期（同＋2.6％）から鈍化。＋3％前後のインフレに割り負けている
状況変わらず。実質金融資産の目減りも相まって、家計の貯蓄率は高止まり
（出所）Eurostat 、米商務省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：ドイツでは防衛・インフラ投資の効果が一部発現。恩恵は年後半以降一段と顕在化
9
 財政拡張を支えに、ドイツでは防衛・インフラ関連業種の生産・受注が拡大
― 生産水準が急速に高まる武器・弾薬の他、足元では鉄道車両の生産も拡大
― 公共部門によるインフラ投資の受注残は直近2四半期で＋10％超の増加
 今後の財政支出余地を踏まえれば、当該業種への恩恵は年後半以降、一段と顕在化する見込み
―6月末時点の執行額は既に2025年通年の約8割に達するも、年間予算に対する執行率は4割弱にとどまる

4～6月期
７月
（前期比、％）
欧州：4～6月期の輸出拡大はエネルギー市況や特需が主因。輸出競争力の低迷は変わらず
10
 エネルギー市況や石化製品の代替調達需要増等を受け、4～6月期は輸出が拡大
―4～6月期の輸出金額は前期比＋5.6％（輸出数量：同＋2.1%）の増加。単価上昇によりエネルギーが急増した他、アジ
ア系メーカーの生産調整を受けた代替調達需要の高まり等を背景に、化学製品が増加
 ただし、底流にある輸出競争力の低迷は変わらず。AI関連財の外需寄与はネットでマイナス
―AI関連財の輸入額は年初来＋27.1％増加し、輸出の伸び（同＋14.0％）を大きく上回る
EU：AI関連財の輸出入

日本：耐久財特需一服後も、所得改善を背景に緩やかな回復基調を維持
16
 耐久財特需の押し上げは一服
― 自動車・エアコン購入の特需は徐々に剥落。食品などの非耐久財は根強い節約志向を背景に弱含み
― 一方、サービス消費は増加基調を維持。個人消費は一時的な上振れから元の緩やかな拡大トレンドへ回帰する動き
 個人消費は良好な所得環境を背景に今後も緩やかな回復基調で推移する見通し
― 実質賃金の伸びは低下も、夏季賞与要因の剥落が原因。実質所定内給与は足元でも1％程度の伸びを維持
― 懸念材料は再び上昇し始めた原油価格。高インフレが再燃すれば、個人消費の回復に水を差す懸念

日本：日銀追加利上げは最短インターバル。今後も日銀は3カ月に1回程度のペースで利上げへ
17
 日銀は9月会合で利上げを決定（反対2名）。利上げは6月以来3カ月ぶりで、今次局面では最も短いインターバル
―9月会合後の会見で植田総裁は、物価押し上げから物価上振れ抑制へと「政策の局面が変化した」と説明
― 政策金利は1.25％と、日銀による中立金利推計値のレンジ下限を上回るが、金融環境は利上げ後も緩和的と評価
 今後も日銀は3カ月に1回程度のペースで利上げを続ける可能性大
― 大幅利上げや連続利上げについては物価上振れリスクが大きい場合の選択肢と説明。一方、急激な政策変更を回避す

る観点から、先手を打って利上げを実施していく姿勢
物価上振れリスクへの対応から
利上げ間隔が短期化
政策金利とブレークイーブン・インフレ
（注） ブレークイーブン・インフレは基調的物価上昇率の一面を捉えていると考えられるが、
必ずしも基調的物価上昇率そのものを示す指標ではない点に留意
（出所）日本銀行、LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成 （出所） 日本銀行より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
日銀9月会合のポイント（声明文・総裁会見）
声明文
• 企業の賃金・価格設定行動の積極化や中長期的な予想物価上昇
率の上昇などを踏まえると、2％物価目標を上振れるリスクがある
• 企業間取引における価格上昇圧力が消費者段階にも波及し始め
ているほか、賃金上昇の販売価格への転嫁の動きも続いている
• 政策金利の変更後も、緩和的な金融環境は維持される
会見での注目発言
• 基調的物価上昇率が2％に接近し、政策局面が変化。物価上振れ
を回避する観点が重要に
• 原油価格の上昇やそこから波及した物価上昇圧力、AI関連需要

や円安進行が幅広い品目の物価上昇圧力に繋がる可能性
• 基調的物価の2％定着を確認するのにはしばらく時間がかかる
• 金融緩和の度合いは利上げにより少しずつ縮まっているが、銀行
貸し出しや資産市場は依然として緩和的
• 急激な利上げで金融環境が大きく変化することや、それによる経
済へのマイナス影響を回避する観点は重要
• 大幅利上げに追い込まれる可能性を減らすように政策を運営する

米国債券市場：インフレ懸念と追加利上げ観測を背景に利回りは上昇
 9月の米10年国債利回りは、インフレ懸念の高まりから5.2％まで上昇
― 月前半は、雇用統計の上振れや原油高、物価指標の高止まりから、長期金利は5％の大台に
― 中旬は、FOMCが25bpの利上げを決定。追加利上げの可能性も示唆され、長期金利は5％前後で高止まり
― 月後半は、堅調な景況感やFRB高官のタカ派発言を背景に、長期金利は5.2％台まで上昇
 今後は、ホルムズ海峡を巡る中東情勢の動向が鍵に。原油高が続けば、複数回の追加利上げにも現実味
― トランプ大統領がイランの提示した停戦案を拒否するなど、中東情勢の先行きは依然として不透明

国内債券・為替：長期金利は一時3.1％超え。ドル円は往って来いで円安継続へ
 長期金利は上昇。一時3.115％と1996年8月ぶりの水準に
― 日銀9月会合前後で利上げ前倒しを織り込む動き。米FRBの追加利上げ観測も、円安進行を通じて金利上昇の要因に
― 先行きも原油価格や米金利の高止まりが見込まれるもと、日本の長期金利も3％超での推移が続く可能性
 9月のドル円は往って来い。先行きも、150円台を中心とした円安水準が続く見込み
― 9月前半は、ベッセント財務長官の円安警戒発言や日銀利上げ観測の高まりを受け、一時152円台後半まで円高に
― もっとも、円高基調は定着せず。9月後半にかけては米利上げ観測の高まりを背景に150円台後半まで再度円安が進行

内外株式：原油高が重石も、AI関連需要を支えに日米株はともに底堅く推移
 日米株は、中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う原油高が重石となるも、AI・半導体関連需要を支えに底堅く推移
― 日経平均は月半ばの調整後、半導体関連株がけん引し上昇。S&P500も最高値圏を維持
―WTI 原油価格は、サウジアラビアの主要パイプライン停止等から一時100ドル超へ急伸。パイプラインの部分的な稼働
再開を受け月半ば以降は反落も、インフレ懸念に伴う金利上昇が株価の重石に
 日経VIは緩やかに低下、米VIXも低位横ばい圏。他方で原油・金利動向を巡る振れやすさには引き続き留意

― 中東情勢を受けた変動は一時的に留まった一方、米・イラン間の協議停滞など原油高が株価を圧迫する構図は残存

## Document 128: 9月PMI数据解读：季末回暖，政策续力

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月PMI数据解读：季末回暖，政策续力.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

事项：
9 月份，制造业 PMI为 50.1%，环比上行 0.3 个百分点；非制造业 PMI为 50.2%，环比上行 1.2个百分点。
评论：
图1：中国 PMI主要数据一览
资料来源：Wind，中采咨询，国信证券经济研究所整理
结论：9 月制造业 PMI上行 0.3个百分点至 50.1，时隔两个月重回荣枯线上，回升幅度略弱于季节性。主
要是生产发力，需求端小幅回落但仍维持在荣枯线上。价格端大幅上行，购进价格上行 4.2 个百分点至
60.8，出厂价格上行 3.6 个百分点至 54.0，预计 PPI 增速将继续抬升。非制造业 PMI 上行 1.2 个百分点
至 50.2，重回荣枯线上。其中建筑业上行 3.4个百分点至 50.3，时隔 8 个月重回扩张区间。整体来看，9
月经济如期回暖，“金九”旺季效应有一定显现。
近期国常会提到的“推出一批务实管用的增量政策”开始逐步落地，后续四季度经济将继续回暖。9月 29
日，财政部、央行、金融监管总局联合发布居民购房贷款贴息政策，央行同日下调 PSL 利率 0.25 个百分

点至 1.5%并将水网、算力网等“六张网”建设纳入支持范围。房贷贴息的看点有二：一是中央财政首次对
商业性个人住房贷款贴息，有较强的信号意义；二是力度较为克制，贴息范围限定新增首套、120 平方米
与 150万元以内、单户上限 100万元，首年财政成本或不到百亿，后续不排除进一步加码的可能。展望四
季度，增量政策密集落地对预期的提振逐渐显现；叠加政策性金融工具加快投放与专项债使用提速，基建

### Page 2

有望边际改善。预计三季度 GDP增速回升到 4.5%，四季度继续回升至 4.7%或以上。
 制造业
9 月制造业景气度重回荣枯线上。9 月制造业 PMI上行 0.3个百分点至 50.1，同比去年高 0.3个百分点，
比近三年同期均值高 0.2个百分点。从环比变化来看，本月回升幅度略小于近三年的季节性回升（环比均
值约 0.5个百分点）。分贡献度看，9 月 PMI的回升几乎全部由生产贡献。按贡献大小分别是生产（贡献
0.325 个百分点）、原料库存（贡献 0.01个百分点）、配送（贡献 0 个百分点）、新订单（贡献-0.03 个
百分点）、雇员（贡献-0.06 个百分点）。
生产大幅回升，需求小幅回落。从相对变化看，“产需差”由负转正：生产回升（+1.3个百分点）、新订
单小幅回落（-0.1个百分点），“产需差”走阔 1.4 个百分点至 1.2个百分点，回到供强需弱的情况。从
绝对水平看，生产上行 1.3个百分点至 51.7，创 2025年 12月以来新高。“三大需求”集体下滑：新订单
（-0.1个百分点至 50.5）仍在荣枯线上，新出口订单（-0.1个百分点至 50.0）恰好位于荣枯线，在手订

单（-0.7个百分点至 46.0）仍在收缩区间。
库存分化，价格大幅上行。库存方面，原材料库存上行 0.1个百分点至 48.2，结束连续四个月下行；产成
品库存下行 0.8个百分点至 47.6，连续两个月下行。与此同时，购进价和产出价均大幅上行。购进价格上
行 4.2个百分点至 60.8，创 2026年 4月以来新高；出厂价格上行 3.6个百分点至 54.0，同样创 2026年 4
月以来新高。价格端上行信号强化，9 月 PPI环比或明显回升。
企业预期持平，小型企业景气度明显回升。制造业生产经营活动预期持平于 53.8，仍在荣枯线上，企业中
长期信心较强。分规模看，大型企业景气度持平于 50.6，连续两个月位于扩张区间；中型企业景气度上行
0.3 个百分点至 49.7，小型企业景气度上行 1.0 个百分点至 48.9，“大小企业差”收窄 1.0 个百分点至
1.7个百分点。17个制造业行业中有 9 个行业 PMI位于荣枯线上，其中专用设备、汽车制造业、农副食品
等中下游行业表现较好，装备制造业和高技术制造业 PMI 分别为 51.0%和 52.5%。

 非制造业
9 月非制造业 PMI上行 1.2个百分点至 50.2，重回荣枯线上，仍弱于近年同期（比近三年同期均值低 0.4
个百分点），但环比表现明显好于近三年同期（均值约 0 个百分点）。结构上，非制造业新订单上行 2.4
个百分点至 46.5，仍处低位；就业人员上行 0.5 个百分点至 45.9，就业景气度仍然偏弱；预期上行 0.5
个百分点至 55.5，仍在荣枯线上。
非制造业价格指标显著抬升。当月价格指标中，购进价格上行 1.4 个百分点至 52.5，出厂价格上行 1.0
个百分点至 50.3、重回荣枯线上，产出与投入的差值下行 0.4 个百分点至-2.2 个百分点，非制造业企业
利润率压力仍然偏大。
行业上，服务业与建筑业双双重回线上。服务业 PMI上行 0.9个百分点至 50.2，时隔两个月重回荣枯线上。
其中，电信广播电视及卫星传输服务、货币金融服务、保险等行业商务活动指数均位于 55.0%以上较高景
气区间；资本市场服务、房地产等行业商务活动指数均低于临界点。具体来看，居民服务下行 2.6 个百分

点到 45-50 之间，交运上行 1.5个百分点到 50-55之间，信息服务下行 0.6 个百分点到 50-55 之间。建筑
业 PMI上行 3.4个百分点至 50.3，时隔 8 个月重回扩张区间，或主要与前期极端天气影响消退、施工进度
加快有关。其中，房建中类上行 1.0个百分点，仍位于 45-50 之间。

### Page 3

## Document 177: August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-09).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
August CPI – Came in on our expectations
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
15:27 September 30 2026 Share
August Monthly CPI: Headline 0.4%mth/4.0%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. Market
services ex volatile –0.4%mth/3.3%yr.

The CPI rose 0.4% in August, spot on Westpac’s expectation but a bit softer than market
expectations of 0.5%. Due to base effects the annual pace picked up to 4.0%yr from 3.5%yr.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline result were auto fuels (14.8%); games, toys & hobbies (10.2%) with
rents providing a solid base (0.3%) while some offset was provided by both domestic (–4.9%) and
international holiday travel & accommodation (–1.5%).
Trimmed mean (TM) inflation printed 0.2% again bang on Westpac’s expectation while the market
was slightly higher at 0.3%. The annual pace was flat at 3.6%yr, the third month in a row at this pace,
while the six-month annualise pace was steady at 3.9%yr.

Key factors driving the TM print were: rents (0.3%) and new dwelling purchases (0.2%). The TM
distribution ranged from –0.38% to 0.82%.
Today’s inflation update revealed price discounting for some durable goods as retail demand softens.
We would expect to see a bounce from these categories in the following month. There was also
offsetting strength elsewhere in durable good so at least for now, the momentum continues to
suggest some near term upside risk to our current inflation estimates.

Nevertheless, as the TM came in on expectations and based on a first quick pass at updating the
quarterly profile, we see little pressure to revise our current Q3 estimates of 1.3%qtr for the CPI and
0.9%qtr for the TM.
The RBA remains alert to near term inflationary risks. We are expecting the momentum in TM
inflation to remain well above the top of the target band through to year-end underpinning our
expectations for the RBA to raise rates again in November.

### Page 3

August CPI in more detail
Following the bumper read in July, the monthly CPI moderated in August rising 0.4%mth. This was in
line with our expectations and slightly softer than the market consensus. This took the year-ended pace
to 4.0%yr. On a seasonally adjusted basis the CPI rose 0.7%mth highlighting the impact of seasonal
sales in August.

They key drivers behind the monthly lift were automotive fuel, up 14.8%mth, contributing 0.5ppts to the
headline figure. The surge resulted from the ending of the fuel excise discount on August 3 and rising
global energy prices. With crude oil prices still elevated due to the conflict in the middle east, and
growing shortages of refined products due to both the middle east conflict and Ukrainian assaults on
Russian refining capacity, auto fuel is expected to remain an inflationary impulse at least until the end
of 2026.

The monthly TM also softened, rising 0.2%mth compared to the 0.5%mth read from July. The result
was again in line with our expectations, and very close at two decimal places (0.24%mth vs
0.23%mth), but softer than the market's view. The year-ended pace held at 3.6%yr for the third
consecutive month, although it continued to ease marginally at the second decimal place. In contrast,
the six-month annualised pace has continued to trend higher since April and is currently running at
3.9%yr.

### Page 4

There is a possibility that as the ABS continues to grapple with seasonal adjustment issues we will see
revisions to the TM estimates. Last July also saw a strong 0.48%mth read which eased to 0.29%mth in
August. This is not too dissimilar from the 0.48%mth and 0.24%mth reads for 2026.

We are expecting core inflation to start to moderate as we get to year end but our current estimate for
the month of December still has the TM running at a 3.3% in the year while the six-month annualised
pace is still a chunky 3.1%. Applying a trim on the seasonally adjusted 3m/3m expenditure classes
yields a TM of 0.9%qtr currently. This is in line with our current Q3 TM estimate.

Other measures of underlying inflation told a similar story, easing on a monthly basis but holding steady
on a year-ended basis. Market services ex volatiles fell –0.4%mth to be 3.3%yr higher. Excluding both
volatiles & holidays saw a 0.2%mth/4.1%yr lift.

Most market services components eased in the month (including on a seasonally adjusted basis) but
annual growth across this group as a whole remained above the RBA's 2-3% target band. The near-
term momentum (three-month annualised) is also quite strong across several components of services.
Of note include deposit and loan facilities (11.2%mth), sports participation (10.9%), clothing repair
(7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other household services (5.2%). We are closely watching market
services as this is where inflationary risks, outside of energy, appear to be most persistent.

Due to its sizable weight in the CPI, and the fact price changes here tend to be less volatile than many
other categories, so they almost always appear in the TM estimates, housing prices remain a key
focus. Rents rose 0.3%mth in August, in line with our expectations, to be up 3.6%yr. Since April, the
seasonally adjusted rents series has been running lower than the non-seasonally adjusted series. We
are expecting rents to hold around this pace till year end before moderating modestly as we move
through 2027.

New dwellings inflation was 0.2%mth in August (softer than our expectation for a 0.4%mth rise). Since
peaking in May, monthly price growth has slowed in each successive month, potentially reflecting
increasing difficulty for developers in passing through higher costs amid softer conditions in established
housing markets and weaker demand in the face of higher interest rates. The annual pace looks to
have topped out. It was at 5.8%yr in June easing to 5.4%yr currently with the three-month annualised
pace at 3.7%. We expect dwelling price inflation to continue easing through to the end of 2027 as
falling established house prices limit the ability of builders to pass on rising construction costs. There is
a medium-term risk that as conditions improve builders may raise prices more aggressively to claw
back margins.

### Page 5

Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated in August. The share of the basket running above 3%yr
eased from 45% to 42%, with the share below 2%yr increasing from 38% to 41%.

### Page 6

Key surprises in the month:
Upside surprises

Downside surprises
Insurance surprised to the upside (0.7%mth vs 0.3%mth) after a run of weaker-than-usual reads over
the past two months.
Motor vehicle repair costs rose a solid 0.8% against our 0.3%mth expectations, with spare parts and
accessories also up 0.3%mth vs –0.4%mth.
Footwear rose 1.7%mth against our expectations for a -2.9%mth decline, the gains were broad-
based across women, men & children.
Household goods surprised to the upside. Household appliances, utensils and tools rose 1.9%mth
against our flat expectations, meanwhile household textiles fell –1.0%mth against our expectations
for a –5.0%mth fall. Furniture and furnishings also fell by less than expected –0.6%mth vs –1.3%mth.
Recreation and culture surprised to the upside (–1.0%mth vs –1.5%mth), owing to a 1.4%mth lift in
other recreation, sport & culture against our expectations for a flat read on the back of an outsized
gain in games, toys & hobbies (10.2%mth vs 2.5%mth). Holiday travel was marginally less weak than
expected (–3.3% vs –3.5%mth).

### Page 7

Food was the key downside miss falling –0.1%mth against our expectations for a 0.2%mth lift. Meat
& seafood (–0.9%mth vs 0.5%mth) and fruit & vegetables (–1.2%mth vs 0.1%mth) were the key
areas of weakness.
Audio, visual and computing equipment and services came in weaker-than-expected (0.7%mth vs
1.2%mth), owing to a softer 0.6%mth lift in computing services (3.0%mth expected). This was partly
balanced by a stronger than expected lift in computing equipment (0.9%mth vs –0.5%mth). The latter

is where we are most likely to see the impact of the AI boom in the CPI, due to computer chip
inflation, other than construction cost inflation for new dwellings.
Motor vehicles came in lower at –0.4%mth vs –0.1%mth which could be the start of price pressures
associated with the rapid expansion of the share of Chinese cars in the Australian market.
Automotive fuel was also slightly softer at 14.8%mth vs 16.7%mth.
Garments and accessories were both softer. Garments fell –1.9%mth against our expectations for a

0.2%mth rise, meanwhile accessories came in at 0.1%mth vs 0.5%mth expected.

### Page 8

What does this mean for the RBA?
Yesterday, post the RBA rate hike and monetary policy decision statement, Westpac changed its view
from a November follow up rate hike being a risk to being the base case. The post-meeting statement
was notable for its emphasis on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices are now
much higher than had been assumed in the August forecasts”. In addition, RBA’s liaison suggests firms
are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-through was characterised as partial, and additional

to domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We read this as implying that the RBA
is concerned about upside risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.

### Page 9

Related articles
Our current forecast of 0.9%qtr for the September quarter TM stands post today’s release and
represents a step up from 0.8% in the June quarter holding the annual pace at 3.6%yr. Our current
forecast for the Q4 TM is 0.7%qtr/3.3%yr but given the move in energy prices, and anecdotes of firms
now passing on those rising costs, we too see upside risks to this estimate.

As such, we hold to the view that the RBA will follow up with another rate hike in November. For more
information on the RBA decision please see Luci Ellis’s note “RBA hikes in September, November
follow-up now expected".

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, 20.45 điểm tương ứng 1.14%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Tiện ích khí là các nhóm ngành có mức tăng rõ nét nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị 2,680 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, HDB và BSR; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở 
mức 1,172 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại mua ròng 3,009 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 5,001 hợp đồng. Tự doanh bán ròng -9,259 hợp đồng và đang duy 
trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng 9,458 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần tăng điểm tích cực, chỉ số VNINDEX chinh phục thành công 
ngưỡng tâm lý 1.800 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện, độ rộng thị trường nghiêng về số mã 
tăng điểm và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài quay trở lại mua ròng. Động lực hỗ trợ thị trường 
trong tuần qua đến từ hoạt động cơ cấu của các quỹ bám theo rổ chỉ số mới nổi của FTSE, 
khi mà ngày 21/9 là ngày Việt Nam được chính thức nâng hạng. Thêm vào đó, diễn biến hạ 
nhiệt của giá dầu khi lo ngại thiếu hụt cung do xung đột ở Trung Đông giảm bớt cũng là 
yếu tố hỗ trợ tâm lý thị trường chung. Ở chiều ngược lại, thông tin FED tăng lãi suất có tác 
động tiêu cực nhưng ảnh hưởng không đáng kể lên thị trường do động thái này đã được 
dự báo từ trước.

### Page 3

3

Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm 
một lần tăng nữa trong năm 
Đánh giá áp lực lên tỷ giá và lãi suất Việt Nam

Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

### Page 4

4

Kết quả kinh doanh 2Q2026 tích cực 
nhờ hưởng lợi từ căng thẳng địa 
chính trị ở Trung Đông

Giá dầu duy trì ở mức cao, tiếp tục hỗ 
trợ biên lợi nhuận khí khô của GAS

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi mở rộng dư địa 
phát triển mỏ cận biên, tạo động lực 
tăng nguồn khí cho GAS trong dài 
hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
96,300 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng trưởng tích cực 44% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ (1) giá bán khí tăng 
và (2) tăng trưởng sản lượng tích cực từ mảng LNG. Trong khi đó, LNST-CĐTS tăng 
chậm hơn, ở mức 23% YoY, do ghi nhận khoản hoàn nhập dự phòng nợ phải thu khó 
đòi thấp hơn so với cùng kỳ năm trước.

Giá dầu Brent tăng trở lại lên vùng 95 USD/thùng trong bối cảnh xung đột Mỹ - Iran 
tiếp tục leo thang, làm gián đoạn nguồn cung dầu tại Trung Đông, trong khi lưu 
lượng tàu qua Eo biển Hormuz vẫn ở mức thấp và tồn kho dầu toàn cầu cùng Kho 
Dự trữ Chiến lược Mỹ liên tục suy giảm. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá dầu duy trì vùng 85 - 
95 USD/thùng trong ngắn hạn, qua đó tiếp tục hỗ trợ biên lợi nhuận mảng khí 
thượng nguồn của GAS nhờ chênh lệch giữa giá bán khí theo giá dầu và giá miệng 
giếng cố định tại các mỏ lâu đời.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi tăng ưu đãi cho các mỏ cận biên, giúp cải thiện hiệu quả đầu 
tư và thúc đẩy phát triển nguồn khí mới. GAS có thể hưởng lợi từ sản lượng khí bổ 
sung qua hệ thống hạ tầng hiện hữu, nhưng tác động chủ yếu trong trung và dài 
hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA để định giá cổ phiếu GAS, chúng tôi đưa 
ra khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 96,300 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức 
upside 16.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Kết quả kinh doanh tích cực trong 
2Q2026

Giá urea hạ nhiệt, xuất khẩu tiếp tục 
là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính 
của DCM trong 2H2026

Giá khí đầu vào DCM 2028–2030F 
tăng do bổ sung nguồn khí giá cao từ 
Nam Du – U Minh

Luật Dầu khí mới cải thiện triển vọng 
nguồn khí đầu vào cho DCM trong 
dài hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
42,100 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng 10% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ giá bán urea tăng trong bối cảnh thị 
trường chịu tác động từ các yếu tố địa chính trị. LNST-CĐTS tăng 36% YoY, được hỗ 
trợ bởi giá bán đầu ra tăng nhanh hơn chi phí khí đầu vào.

Giá urea thế giới sau khi tăng cao trong Q2/2026 đã điều chỉnh về vùng trước chiến 
tranh do nhu cầu suy yếu và nguồn cung Trung Quốc quay trở lại. Đối với DCM, xuất 
khẩu urea tiếp tục là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính, trong khi giá và sản lượng 
urea nội địa được kỳ vọng đi ngang trong 2H2026 do đây không phải mùa cao điểm 
tiêu thụ trong nước.

Chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng 10% dự báo giá khí đầu vào DCM giai đoạn 2028–2030F, 
phản ánh việc bổ sung nguồn khí Nam Du – U Minh từ Q4/2028. Giá khí Nam Du – U 
Minh dự kiến 11.56 USD/mmBTU, cao hơn ~35% so với các nguồn hiện hữu, tạo áp 
lực tăng chi phí đầu vào.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi sẽ rút ngắn quy trình phê duyệt FDP và bổ sung ưu đãi cho các 
mỏ khó khai thác, qua đó hỗ trợ đầu tư E&P và duy trì sản lượng dầu khí trong 
nước. Đối với DCM, tác động dự kiến mang tính gián tiếp và có độ trễ, chủ yếu thông 
qua cải thiện nguồn cung khí nội địa, chưa phải động lực thúc đẩy lợi nhuận đáng 
kể trong ngắn hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu DCM chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 42,100 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
29.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Doanh thu và LNST-CĐTS 2Q2026 
tiếp tục tăng trưởng tốt, được hỗ trợ 
bởi đà tăng của crack spread

Biên lợi nhuận duy trì ở mức cao 
trong ngắn hạn nhờ crack spread 
bùng nổ, nhưng có thể biến động 
mạnh

Biên lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn chịu 
áp lực từ rủi ro dư cung dầu

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
32,200 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Q2/2026, doanh thu BSR tăng 60% YoY nhờ giá nhiên liệu tăng mạnh trong 
bối cảnh nguồn cung gián đoạn do xung đột Trung Đông. LNST-CĐTS tăng 807% 
YoY, được hỗ trợ bởi crack spread của bốn sản phẩm lọc dầu chủ lực tăng mạnh do 
nguồn cung thắt chặt.

Xung đột quân sự Mỹ – Iran gây gián đoạn nghiêm trọng dòng chảy dầu qua Eo biển 
Hormuz, thắt chặt nguồn cung dầu thô và thành phẩm, đẩy giá Brent lên quanh 100 
USD/thùng và đưa crack spread tại châu Á đạt đỉnh và duy trì ở vùng cao từ tháng 3 
đến nay. Diễn biến crack spread thời gian tới sẽ phụ thuộc đáng kể vào diễn biến 
chiến sự tại Eo biển Hormuz và tốc độ khôi phục lưu thông qua Eo biển này.

Triển vọng lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn của BSR chịu áp lực khi cán cân cung – cầu 
dầu toàn cầu được dự báo chuyển sang dư cung trong trung và dài hạn. Tuy nhiên, 
nhu cầu tái tích lũy tồn kho dầu toàn cầu có thể phần nào hấp thụ lượng cung dư 
thừa và hạn chế mức độ thu hẹp crack spread.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu BSR chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị Mua với mức giá mục tiêu 32,200 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
16.9% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026. Chúng tôi áp dụng mức EV/EBITDA mục 
tiêu là 15.8 lần, bằng với mức chuẩn trung bình 5 năm của cổ phiếu.

BÁO CÁO NGẮN CƠ HỘI ĐẦU TƯ

15/09/2026

CTCP Nhựa Tiền Phong (NTP) 
Tăng tốc trong chu kỳ thuận lợi

Mua 
Giá mục tiêu VND 73.200 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 1 50.000 – 51.400 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 1 44% 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 2 42.000 – 44.000 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 2 70%

Lưu ý: 
Nhà đầu tư cần kết hợp nhận định xu hướng thị 
trường chung, mức độ hấp dẫn của các điểm 
nhấn đầu tư và khẩu vị rủi ro của mình để quản 
lý tỷ trọng tại từng điểm mua cho phù hợp. 
Do việc xác định mỗi điểm mua được dựa trên 
các khung thời gian khác nhau nên chúng tôi 
không đưa ra mức dừng lỗ cố định. Nhìn chung, 
mức dừng lỗ nên được đặt chặt hơn cho điểm 
mua gần (5-7%) và rộng hơn cho điểm mua xa 
(10-15%).

ĐIỂM NHẤN ĐẦU TƯ 
Kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ tăng trưởng mạnh hơn so với giai đoạn trước. KBSV 
dự báo sản lượng tiêu thụ của NTP tăng 2,3% CAGR giai đoạn 2026 - 2028 nhờ (1) 
Đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư hạ tầng tại miền Bắc (2) gia tăng đầu tư hạ tầng tại các 
địa phương thu hút FDI (3) tăng tốc đơn hàng mảng dự án.

Giá hạt nhựa sẽ gây áp lực biên lợi nhuận nửa cuối 2026 nhưng không làm thay 
đổi triển vọng dài hạn. Mặc dù duy trì chính sách dự phòng nguyên liệu cao và đa 
dạng hóa nhà cung cấp, nhưng NTP vẫn đối mặt với rủi ro thu hẹp lợi nhuận, 
nhưng sẽ không làm giảm đáng kể triển vọng tăng trưởng trung hạn. KBSV cho 
rằng tình trạng dư cung hạt nhựa tại Trung Quốc vẫn sẽ duy trì trong trung hạn và 
xung đột được giải quyết, giảm áp lực biên lợi nhuận toàn ngành.

Catalyst thoái vốn kỳ vọng tái định giá doanh nghiệp. Theo kế hoạch thoái vốn 
Nhà nước, SCIC dự kiến thoái toàn bộ 37,1% cổ phần tại NTP . Chúng tôi đánh giá 
việc hiện thực hóa thương vụ có thể giúp NTP (1) thu hút nhà đầu tư chiến lược 
mới; (2) cộng hưởng mô hình kinh doanh; (3) tái định giá doanh nghiệp.

Hoạt động kinh doanh  Diễn biến doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất

NTP là một trong những doanh nghiệp đầu 
ngành ống nhựa tại Việt Nam, với thị phần 
khoảng 31% toàn quốc và 60% tại miền Bắc. 
Hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi tập trung vào 
sản xuất và phân phối ống nhựa cùng các sản 
phẩm phụ tùng. Nhờ lợi thế về thương hiệu, 
quy mô và mạng lưới phân phối, NTP duy trì 
vị thế dẫn đầu và nền tảng tăng trưởng ổn 
định qua các năm.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KB Securities Vietnam

Tương quan mặt bằng giá  Diễn biến P/E của doanh nghiệp

phiếu. Với kỳ vọng doanh nghiệp sẽ bước vào 
pha tăng trưởng mạnh trong 3 năm tới, chúng

Tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 được dự 
báo khả quan so với cùng kỳ

NIM chịu áp lực trước mục tiêu tăng 
trưởng

Chất lư ợng tài s ản suy gi ảm nh ẹ do 
mặt bằng lãi suất ở mức cao

Khuyến ngh ị: MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
25,950 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín d ụng 2026 đ ạt 27%, với động lực chính 
đến từ phân khúc KHDN, đ ặc biệt là t ừ nhóm FDI trong b ối cảnh dòng v ốn FDI vào 
Việt Nam và hoạt động xuất nhập khẩu duy trì tích cực. Xét về lĩnh vực, cho vay kinh 
doanh BĐS kỳ vọng sẽ tăng trưởng vượt trội khi ngân hàng tham gia vào thu xếp vốn 
cho các dự án hạ tầng và d ự án BĐS ưu tiên , trong khi các m ảng kinh doanh khác 
tăng trưởng ở mức khả quan.

Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng NIM giảm so với năm 2025 do áp l ực huy động khiến chi 
phí vốn duy trì ở mặt bằng cao, trong khi lãi su ất cho vay dự kiến ở mức cạnh tranh 
để ưu tiên mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín dụng.

Chúng tôi dự phóng tỷ lệ nợ xấu của ngân hàng sẽ tăng 11bps YoY trong bối cảnh lãi 
suất đang neo ở mức cao và kỳ vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì chính sách trích 
lập dự phòng thận trọng nhằm củng cố bộ đệm, qua đó hoàn thành mục tiêu duy 
trì tỷ lệ bao phủ nợ xấu ở mức trên 100%.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi tiếp tục duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với cổ phiếu MBB với giá mục tiêu 25,950 
VNĐ/CP - tiềm năng tăng giá 26.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 17/09/2026.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX cho thấy xu hướng hồi phục trong tuần qua nhưng với diễn biến khá giằng co, 
được thể hiện qua sự hình thành của một mẫu nến "spinning" trên đồ thị tuần. Trên khung 
ngày, bức tranh có phần tích cực hơn khi chỉ số đã cho phản ứng với vùng hỗ trợ quanh 
1790 và hồi phục trở lại, phản ánh sự nhập cuộc khá chủ động của lực cầu. Vùng kháng cự 
gần quanh 1850 bắt đầu khiến áp lực bán có phần gia tăng trong phiên cuối tuần và diễn 
biến rung lắc có thể sẽ còn tiếp diễn. Mặc dù vậy, khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được 
bảo lưu, nhịp giảm điểm vừa qua nhiều khả năng chỉ là bước điều chỉnh tích lũy trước khi 
VNINDEX thử thách lại và có cơ hội vượt vùng đỉnh cũ. 
Kháng cự xa: 1900 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1850 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1770 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1715 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 211: Cliff Notes_ the importance of supply (2026-10-10)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ the importance of supply (2026-10-10).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-10
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the importance of supply
By Elliot Clarke, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
09:47 September 25 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s only data release this week was the August Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised to
the upside, rising 39.5k. But, once again, new labour demand was more than offset by supply as the
participation rate bounced 0.2ppts higher to 67.1%, just shy of January 2025’s 115-year high of 67.2%.

As a result, the unemployment rate increased from 4.5% to 4.6% and was close to rounding up to 4.7%
(4.65% to two decimal places).

### Page 2

There may be some residual seasonality in August’s print related to the new survey collection model.
However, the underlying trend is still consistent with a gradual easing in the labour market, seeing the
unemployment rate drift higher. We expect this to continue through to the first half of 2027.
The strength in labour force participation also speaks to the broader issue of Australia’s supply
capacity. As discussed by Chief Economist Luci Ellis, the RBA’s assumptions around participation,

productivity and the NAIRU collectively skew towards a weak assessment of supply capacity. For
participation in particular, the RBA’s near-term assumptions are downbeat compared to recent
demographic trends and also sit below Treasury’s longer-term projections as set out in the 2026 IGR.
As above, the August LFS emphasises that slack could emerge more quickly than the RBA’s forecasts
imply without an outright fall in employment.
Ahead of next week’s RBA meeting and inflation data, our latest state-level analysis provides

perspective on the persistence of underlying price pressures, particularly the importance of housing-
related components. For a broader assessment of the fiscal backdrop and the implications for the
states, our latest state fiscal update explores how the housing market correction and changes to NDIS
funding arrangements are pressuring state finances.
Before turning to this week’s events offshore, it is important to highlight that last Friday the Bank of

Japan raised its policy rate by 25bp to 1.25%. Policymakers maintained a constructive assessment of
the economy, pointing to resilient consumption, ongoing labour market tightness, and solid investment
spending. More importantly, the Bank expressed growing confidence that inflation is increasingly being
sustained by domestic wage and demand dynamics rather than imported cost shocks alone.
In our view, the emphasis placed on improving underlying inflation dynamics suggests policymakers

are becoming increasingly comfortable that inflation is able to be sustained by domestic demand and
wage growth. Normalisation is likely to remain gradual, but the Bank appears firmly on a path towards
further tightening, with rates likely to reach 1.5% by mid-2027.
This week, market participants have focused on discussions between the US and China on trade,
security and technology as President Xi visits Washington.
Preliminary discussions between US Treasury Secretary Bessent and Chinese Vice Premier He Lifeng

ahead of President Xi’s arrival in Washington were characterised by both sides as productive, and
Treasury Secretary Bessent subsequently announced that discussions in Washington would leave
trade negotiations for upcoming meetings between the two leaders in Shenzhen and Miami. To allow
room for this deferral, the current trade truce will be extended until January 2027. President Trump
subsequently outlined that discussions during President Xi’s Washington visit would instead focus on

security, technology and AI.
A more constructive tone was also seen between the US and Iran overnight, if press reports are
accurate. According to Bloomberg and Reuters, sources suggest the US and Iran are again exploring a
staged deal which would see Iran re-open the Strait of Hormuz and the US lift its blockade of Iranian
ports. Negotiations are occurring on the sidelines of the UN General Assembly, with Qatari officials

### Page 3

Related articles
mediating.
President Trump continues to show little interest in a deal before November’s mid-term elections, but
the global economy is under increasing pressure as the price of Brent oil hovers between USD100 and
USD110 while refining spreads add additional impetus to refined product prices. The mooted
consideration of a potential US diesel export ban this week makes clear that the US is not immune
from this pressure despite having significant domestic energy supply and refining capacity, relative to

their own demand.
The consequences of the current energy supply shock and other disruptions the US have experienced
over the past year remain front of mind for FOMC members. Those speaking this week such as NY and
Cleveland Fed Presidents Williams and Hammack highlighted the risk to inflation expectations from a
series of supply shocks when the economy is fully employed, and the likelihood of an additional hike(s)
being required to rein in current inflation momentum and risks. Williams noted that expectations of

another hike by year end are "reasonable" and that the FOMC have "a lot of work to do". Debt market’s
concern remains acute, having now almost priced in four hikes over the coming year and taken the US
10-year above 5.00%, currently 5.20%.

## Document 301: Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

23 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
NEXT GENERATION EU: THE PROGRAMME IS COMING TO AN END, BUT ITS EFFECTS ON ECONOMIC ACTIVITY WILL
ENDURE
Guillaume Derrien
A pillar of the NextGenerationEU programme, the Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) will expire on December 31, 2026. As of the
end of August 2026, slightly more than three-quarters of the total budget had been disbursed (EUR 440 billion out of EUR 573 billion).
Disbursing the remaining quarter (EUR 133 billion) by the end of the year will require a significant increase compared with the histo-

rical pace (EUR 90 billion per year). The goal of channeling these funds toward productive investment appears to have been achieved.
Tangible results for investment in Europe
According to Eurostat, public spending co-financed by the RRF totaled EUR 310 billion between 2020 and 2025. Nearly three-quarters (74%)
consisted of capital expenditures, including EUR 106 billion in direct public investment and EUR 122 billion in capital transfers to the private

sector (such as grants). This spending supported investment by businesses and households.
The list of major programmes funded by the RFF, published by the European Commission 1, shows that, within this scope, nearly two-thirds of
the funding went to transportation (primarily rail), energy infrastructure, and digital infrastructure (see Chart 1).
Another notable point: capital expenditures co-financed by the RRF increased significantly, reaching 0.4% of GDP for the European Union as a

whole in 2025 (see Chart 2). There are significant disparities among Member States, primarily due to differences in the amounts allocated to
them. Croatia (1.9%), Greece (1.2%), Italy (1%), and Spain (0.7%) received significant support, in contrast to France and Germany (0.1%). These
figures are consistent with the IMF’s estimate that the RRF added approximately 0.3 percentage points (pp) to EU growth in 2025 (more than
1 pp in Greece and Croatia, around 0.8 pp in Italy, and 0.5 pp in Spain) 2. Furthermore, as the first major joint European borrowing initiative, the

RRF made it possible to finance investment without increasing national budget deficits, giving several countries (Spain, Italy, Greece, Portugal)
valuable breathing room to consolidate their public finances.
1 Recovery and Resilience Scoreboard
2 The EU’s Recovery and Resilience Facility: Experience and Lessons for the Next Multiannual Financial Framework, WP/26/114, June 2026
SOURCE: EUROPEAN COMMISSION, Recovery and Resilience Scorecard

What impact on EU’s economic activity can we expect once this programme ends?
At first glance, effects will be limited. The end of the programme is expected to weigh on both public and private investment. However, the
strengthening of economic activity that we anticipate (see details in our latest Ecoperspectives – Q3 2026) rests primarily on German public

investment programmes and structural drivers (investment in AI, defence spending).
The ECB makes the same point in its September macroeconomic projections 3. It emphasises that the end of the RRF is expected to weigh
on public investment in 2027, while it anticipates an acceleration in business investment. Furthermore, RRF funds are being absorbed on
the ground only gradually. Since most disbursements are concentrated toward the end of the programme, the effects of the RRF will extend

beyond 2026.

The Week Ahead October 9, 2026

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Monetary Authority of Singapore (MAS)

We expect another slight increase in the 
SGD NEER policy band slope at the MAS’ 
October review, with no change to the 
band’s width or centre. Import cost 
pressures and resilient growth favour a 
calibrated tightening, rather than a more 
forceful adjustment, in line with the 
global monetary policy stance.

Also due to be released alongside the 
MAS’ policy decision are the 3Q26 
advance GDP estimates, which we 
expect to show another quarter of strong 
growth at 5.4% yoy (1.4% qoq sa), 
compared with 5.9% yoy (1.4% qoq sa) in 
2Q26. Robust economic performance 
continued to be driven by artificial 
intelligence ( AI)-led trade activity, 
supporting the manufacturing and 
wholesale trade sectors, while strength 
in the financial sector underpinned the 
resilience of the modern services cluster. 
This came despite a moderation of the 
transport & storage sector, and 
weakness in consumer -facing food 
services and retail sectors.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect Malaysia’s advance GDP 
estimate for 3Q26 to register above -
trend growth of 5.0% yoy, albeit 
moderating from 6.0% yoy  in 2Q26. The 
overall expansion continued to be 
supported by ongoing strength in the 
export-oriented manufacturing sector, 
underpinned by strong  AI-related 
demand for electronics, and sustained 
domestic demand, backed by healthy 
investment and private consumption 
trends. However, contraction in mining 
and weak agricultural activity dragged 
overall growth.

Singapore

We expect firm Singapore non -oil 
domestic exports (NODX) growth of 28.0% 
yoy in September 2026, although 
moderating from August’s exceptional 
surge of 46.2%. Although favourable 
base effects largely faded in September, 
electronics shipments likely remained 
the primary growth driver, underpinned 
by robust AI -related demand amid 
sustained global investment in AI 
infrastructure, while non-electronics 
exports were uneven.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 9, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

China

Exports growth is expected to remain 
robust at 25.7% y oy in September, 
supported by continued strength in 
electronics demand. Both the official and 
Caixin manufacturing PMIs rose above 
the 50 expansion threshold during the 
month, pointing to improving 
manufacturing activity. External trade 
indicators also stren gthened, with 
average daily deadweight tonnage 
handled at China's 20 major ports 
accelerating from -0.7% yoy in August to 
7.2% in September. Freight activity 
improved as well, as growth in 
international cargo flights picked up 
from -1.4% y oy to 3.6% over the same 
period.

On the price front, factory -gate and raw 
material prices rebounded amid the 
renewed escalation in geopolitical 
tensions. Main raw material purchasing 
price and producer price subPMI 
increased from 56.6 and 50.4 to 60.8 and 
54.0 during the period. However, 
downstream consumer price pressures 
are likely to remain relatively subdued 
under the pricing-band mechanism. As a 
result, CPI inflation is expected to edge 
up from 0.8% y oy in August to 1.0% in 
September. Nevertheless, soft domestic 
demand is expected to continue 
constraining the pace of consumer price 
increases.

India

September inflation is likely to gather 
momentum to 5.7% y/y, partly driven by 
base effects, from 4.8% the month 
before. Food segments continue to 
register gains, with pressures 
broadening to include perishables, 
edible oils, rice, pulses, sugar, etc., 
according to high-frequency data. Under 
non-food, retail pump prices have not 
been adjusted further, though other fuel 
products, such as non -subsidised LPG, 
diesel and ATF (still up by double digits 
y/y, though slower than in August), are 
up on the year. Core  inflation is also 
likely to tick up, aligning with the central 
bank's view that price risks are no longer 
benign. Pressures have built up due to 
the overhang of an uneven and sub -par 
monsoon, drought conditions in parts of 
the country, and elevated oil pr ices. In 
the face of higher headline prints, we 
expect the RBI to keep the door open for 
another rate hike in December 2026.

## Document 360: United States _ A shock-resistant expansion (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/United States _ A shock-resistant expansion (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
A SHOCK-RESISTANT EXPANSION
US growth remains resilient despite successive shocks. According to our forecasts, it is expected to reach 2.2% in 2026 and
2.3% in 2027. Its main driver is still investment in artificial intelligence (AI), which has remained entirely unaffected by the
energy crisis. The labour market has seen a marked improvement in job creation and a fall in the unemployment rate. Core

inflation remains stubbornly high and above the Federal Reserve (Fed)’s target. Against this backdrop, the Fed has begun a
hiking cycle in September, which, according to our scenario, would bring the Fed Funds target to 4.50% (upper limit) by Q1
2027. At the same time, long-term rates are under upward pressure.
1  This includes: energy and communications; data centres (facilities); IT equipment and peripherals; communication equipment (equipment); software; and research and develop-

ment (intellectual-property products).
THE ENERGY SHOCK IS NOT HOLDING BACK GROWTH
Driven by AI-related investment, US growth is holding up despite ri -
sing energy inflation. We forecast GDP growth at 2.2% in 2026 and
2.3% in 2027 on an annual average basis. The AI-related investment
cycle1 (+14.8% y/y in H1 2026) is expected to continue, accompanied by
potential productivity gains. However, other private-investment seg -
ments (including residential property) remain sluggish. In addition, the

rising cost of capital could accentuate its ‘K-shaped’ profile. Household
consumption is expected, on the whole, to benefit from the rebound in
job creation and to remain above 2% y/y through to the end of 2027.
In H1 2026, it held up against resurging inflation due to an average
year-on-year increase of 11.5% in tax refunds and a savings rate stan-
ding at its lowest level since 2022 (2.8% in Q2).
RECORD CAPITAL-RAISING DRIVEN BY AI
This year, AI-related investments are driving massive capital-raising

by companies through loans, bond issues or share placements. This
trend is set to continue given the seemingly colossal scale of the fun -
ding requirements, even as risk premia in the bond market begin to
widen. According to their statements, the five tech giants could invest
nearly USD 900 bn this year and over USD 1,000 bn next year—more
than double the figure for 2025. Spurred on by regulatory easing, the
major US banks have announced that they will mobilise significant re-

sources to support projects considered strategic for the United States
(AI, defence, energy and infrastructure). While funding for the AI boom
appears unaffected by rising long-term interest rates, these latter are
likely to continue to weigh on household demand for mortgages. Since
the start of the conflict in the Middle East, 30-year mortgage rates
have risen by 78 bp (to 6.76% at the start of September). Conversely,
consumer lending remained buoyant in H1 2026 and is supporting

household spending.
TWO-SPEED GROWTH IN INDUSTRY
Industrial production rebounded slightly in 2025 (+1.1%), driven by AI
and aerospace. We forecast an acceleration to +1.5% in 2026 and +2%
in 2027. Investment in AI is bolstering the electronics sector, while the
(civil and military) aerospace sector is rebounding as order backlogs
are cleared and defence requirements rise. Conversely, sectors ex -
posed to rising tariffs (in particular, the steel, aluminium and auto -

motive sectors) are still on the back burner, contrary to the objective
of these measures. This positive momentum in industrial production,
while concentrated, is expected to continue, bolstered by the recove -
ry in business sentiment. The ISM Manufacturing Index has been in
expansionary territory since early 2026, despite ongoing pressures on
prices and supply chains.

EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
LABOUR MARKET: A LOW UNEMPLOYMENT RATE ON A DOWNWARD
TREND
Job creation (non-farm payrolls) has rebounded, averaging +80,000 per
month since the start of the year, compared with +10,000 in 2025. This
growth is no longer limited to the healthcare sector. Excluding health-
care, job creation is up by an average of +38,000 in 2026, compared

with -47,000 in 2025. Manufacturing and construction, two sectors
driven by AI, are contributing to this rebound. At the same time, the
labour force has not grown since January 2025 due to the decline in the
foreign-born workforce (down 4.2% cumulatively over the period). Ex -
cluding the impact of COVID-19, the labour force participation rate is at
its lowest level since the 1970s, standing at around 61.5%. The rebound
in labour demand and persistent pressure on the labour supply have

brought the unemployment rate down to 4.1% in August 2026 (a 0.3 pp
fall since the start of the year). The jobless rate is expected to fall to
3.9% at the end of 2027 (4.2% corresponds to full employment, accor -
ding to the CBO) thanks to a job creation rate of around 40,000–60,000
per month.
INFLATION: STRUCTURALLY ABOVE TARGET
The rise in oil prices has triggered a rebound in CPI inflation. The
contribution from the energy component (+1.2 pp) largely accounts for

the rise in inflation since February. A slowdown was seen (from +4.2%
to +3.4% y/y) between the May peak and August. This is expected to
continue due to base effects on energy prices. We anticipate the trough
to be +1.8% y/y in Q2 2027 before a stabilisation at around +2.3% y/y
later in the year.
Meanwhile, core inflation is expected to remain above the Fed’s 2%
target, and above +2.5% y/y in 2027. This highlights the structural
nature of inflation, beyond short-term fluctuations linked to energy.

Non-housing services are expected to show significant inertia, with in-
flation hovering at around +3% y/y by the end of 2027. The potential for
disinflation also appears limited for housing-related services, as the
rise in rents shows no signs of abating. Finally, inflation on non-energy
goods is slowing as the effects of tariffs fade. However, the AI cycle is
exerting upward pressure through the general rise in the cost of elec -
tronic goods.
FED: GREEN LIGHT FOR RATE HIKES

The FOMC has embarked on a cycle of rate increases. The Fed Funds
target was raised to 4.0% (+25 bp) at the September meeting, and we
anticipate two further rises that would bring the policy rate to 4.5% in
January 2027. These three rises (made possible by reassuring develop-
ments in employment and economic activity) are intended to reverse
the three cuts in 2025 adopted to counter the ‘downside risks’ to the
labour market at that time. As a result, the committee can recalibrate

its policy towards ‘price stability’ and, through its interest rate deci -
sions, reflect the priority given to this objective in its communications.
The risks point towards further monetary tightening: in our baseline
scenario, core inflation would remain sticky and the fall in unemploy -
ment would give the FOMC room for manoeuvre to raise rates further.
PUBLIC FINANCES: UNDERLYING DETERIORATION AND PRESSURE ON
LONG- TERM RATES
According to our estimates, the federal deficit is set to widen by 0.7 pp

to -6.6% of GDP in 2026 (by 0.4 pp to -3.1% for the primary deficit),
weighed down by falling tariff revenue (refunds of tariffs already col -
lected and lower-than-expected receipts). In 2027, the end of this one-
off effect is expected to lead to a marginal improvement to -6.3% (to
-2.8% for the primary deficit). The growing burden of interest payments
(3.5% of GDP in 2026 and 3.6% in 2027, following 3.2% in 2025) and
the conflict in Iran (affecting the military budget) are weighing on the

budget balance and federal-debt dynamics. Federal debt is still on an
upward trend, with no particular change in direction, exceeding 100% of
GDP (101.9%, up 3.3 pp, in 2026, and 103.5% in 2027). It is expected to
reach 110% by the end of the decade against a backdrop of budget de-
ficits of close to 6% of GDP , assuming fiscal policy remains unchanged.
Furthermore, the mid-term elections (which see the entire House of
Representatives and one-third of the Senate up for re-election) could

result in a divided Congress or in both chambers changing hands to
the Democratic Party. Should the former occur, this would increase the
risk of a legislative deadlock, while the presidential veto would limit
the Democrats’ ability to influence fiscal policy in the latter scenario.
Against this backdrop, pressure on the bond market is likely to continue
in the coming months. Treasury issuance is rising, fuelled by increa -
sing spending requirements (in areas such as AI, defence and supply

chains). At the same time, shifts in investor demographics are increa-
sing demand volatility, and issuances by companies in the artificial
intelligence sector are intensifying competition for capital. We antici -
pate a 10-year yield of 4.95% in Q1 2027, before a slight easing brought
about by Fed rate rises, which would lend credibility to the price-sta -
bility objective. However, according to our forecast, 10-year yield is
expected to remain close to a 20-year high, at 4.80% in Q4 2027.

THE US RELIES ON IMPORTS FOR ITS GROWTH
The US trade deficit has recently started to rise again. According to
our forecasts, it is expected to account for the same proportion of GDP
in 2026 as it did in 2024 (4.4%). This trend is driven by imports of
AI-related capital goods, which are set to rise from 1.2% to 2.1% of GDP
between 2022 and 2026. Investment in AI is, in fact, import-intensive
(Chart 2), which dampens its positive impact on growth and highlights

the US’s dependence on foreign supplies in a cycle that it is neverthe-
less spearheading.
The additional tariffs have failed to reverse the trend in the trade de -
ficit. Furthermore, the IEEPA tariffs struck down by the Supreme Court
were replaced, in July 2026, by ‘Section 301’ tariffs (forced labour) ran-
ging from 10% to 12.5%. These tariffs target 60 countries and have
been introduced on top of existing sector-specific tariffs (steel, alumi -

nium and automotive). Following these adjustments, the statutory rate
(measured against the 2024 import structure) remained slightly above
10%. The main risks relate to potential new sector-specific tariffs and
the renegotiation of the USMCA.
Article completed on 17 September 2026

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
• The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index* has edged up slightly since late
August, rising 0.2pts to 159.1.
• Quarterly growth momentum has
seen a further lift and is now tracking
towards a 1.7% gain for the September
quarter which would mark the strongest

quarterly rise since June 2025.
• However, both the monthly and weekly
reads show momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter. After posting
0.7-0.9%mth gains through May, June
and July, the Index rose just 0.2%
in August and is tracking a similarly
subdued 0.3% pace for September.
Weekly reads are also tracking a more
moderate trend pace of 0.2%mth.
• On a quarterly basis, the detail shows
discretionary services and international
transactions have led the pick-up.

While some of this looks to be catch-
up from travel disruptions in the June
quarter, gains have been consistent and
broadly based. Likewise, there is little
evidence that the pick-up is coming from
a lift in prices with recent CPI updates
suggesting services inflation has been
steady rather than accelerating (see
here).
• On the apparent loss of momentum
over the course of the quarter, the detail
suggests this is a re-run of the fuel price
squeeze in March-April-May with the

essentials spend gain rising strongly. So
far the spillover effects are showing up
mainly as a dampening in discretionary
goods spend with the slowing in
discretionary services largely confined to
hospitality.
• Overall, the card tracker data suggests
the Q3 spending data will still come in
as a relatively solid gain, even allowing
for a firmer gain in prices in the quarter.
However, the fade in momentum is
notable with the impact of further fuel

pricer increases and this week’s RBA rate
rise still to come. Add what now looks
more likely to be a follow-on interest
rate hike in November and the December
quarter is shaping as a significant
slowdown in card activity.
“... both the monthly
and weekly reads show
momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter.”

WESTPAC ECONOMICS 9
About the Westpac card data indicators
The indicators presented in this report are based on the millions of credit and debit card
transactions processed every day. Transactions covering over ten million merchants are
classified into over 700 categories. These are in turn grouped into higher level aggregates

that provide a timely guide to wider economic trends.
The main focus of these indicators is consumer spending. Where possible, we have sought
to exclude ‘non spending’ transactions such as: money transfers; tax payments; loan
repayments; charitable donations; and superannuation contributions.
It should also be noted that these indicators will also be affected by shifts between card and
non-card transactions. This was a significant factor during the COVID–19 pandemic – health

concerns about the use of physical cash leading to significantly higher use of cards vs cash,
particularly where contact–less transactions are available. Transaction flows also include
reversals/refunds which were also a significant phenomenon during the onset of COVID,
especially in areas such as travel.
All transaction data is compiled at a highly aggregated level so that individual customer or
merchant data is never revealed.
Index construction

The key metrics used in this report are indexes of spending–related card activity where the
base of 100 is average activity in 2019. As an example, if transaction flows are 5% above their
average level in 2019, the index read for the period is 105. If flows in a subsequent period are
8% above the average level in 2019, the index read for this period is 108. Growth between the
two periods can be calculated simply as the change between the two index reads, i.e. 2.9%.

All measures are adjusted for regular seasonality. Weekly estimates are generated using
the US Bureau of Labor’s MoveReg weekly seasonal adjustment program. Note that in some
cases, high levels of volatility during the COVID period mean it is not possible to produce
seasonally adjusted estimates for some historical periods.
Also, note that previous versions of this report used different approaches to seasonal
adjustment and measurement more generally. This means Index reads are sometimes not

directly comparable. See the ‘About the Westpac card data indicators’ sections from earlier
reports to more detail.
Classifications
Note that the measures used for card data and in this report do not align completely with
the those used in official ABS statistics, including the ABS household spending indicator,
ABS retail trade survey and ABS estimate of consumer spending published in the national
accounts. There are a range of differences including around both coverage and classification.

As such, the card data should be treated as broadly indicative.
The transaction data is grouped into 26 categories that are then combined into four main as
follows:
Discretionary goods: alcoholic beverages, tobacco, clothing & footwear, furnishings &
household equipment, household appliances, vehicle–related, recreational & cultural goods,
newspaper, books & stationery, and other personal effects.
Discretionary services: transport services (part), recreational & cultural services, gambling,

catering services, accommodation services, other personal care, insurance & financial
services, other services.
Essential goods: food & non alcoholic beverages, medical products, appliances & equipment,
and operation of personal transport equipment.
Essential services: electricity, gas & other fuels, health services, transport services (part),
communications and education.
The report also uses two additional classifications:
MHSI/Retail: based on the extent to which categories cover activity that is in scope for the

ABS monthly household spending indicator and ABS retail trade survey.

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投

東協維持整理，印度新契機浮現
分析師／王聖堯
研究範疇／東協/印度
東南亞投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/10/1
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《東協尚未擺脫整理，美印關係改善訊號浮現》
9月MSCI東協指數下跌 2.6%，雖然全球公債殖利率
與油價走升帶來評價面壓力 ，但從股價跌幅及外資
賣超規模來看 ，本波修正仍偏向短線漲多後的技術
性調節。修正後，東協股市本益比已由高於近兩年
平均2倍標準差回落至約 1倍標準差 ，RSI亦降至40
以下，顯示市場過熱情況明顯改善 。然而在聯準會
升息及殖利率維持高檔的背景下 ，若基本面未能提
供更多上修空間 ，預期股市短期仍將維持震盪整理
格局。印度方面，9月回檔已使股價回測 3月低點，
本益比降至 19.9倍，RSI跌破30，顯示短線賣壓已

有過度反應。此外，莫迪於9月金磚峰會公開反對去
美元化，近期川普亦表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，反映
美印經貿關係出現改善訊號 。後續可持續關注雙邊
合作進展及其對印度中長期投資影響。
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望
東南亞 中性 估值及RSI已回到相對合理位置，但殖利率比原先預期更高，基本面需要看到優於預期的表現才有上調空間。

《外資9月轉為小幅賣超，呼應8月轉中立看法》
8月下調東協評等至中立 ，主要考量 6至8月股市累
積漲幅已大致反映第三季經濟維持穩健成長的預期 ，
在缺乏新的催化劑下 ，市場進一步上行空間受限 。
進入9月後，美國公債殖利率與國際油價同步走升，
使市場重新評估高利率與成本壓力對企業獲利的影
響，並加大非科技類股及非科技市場的修正幅度 。
不過，相較於美國公債殖利率持續創高所帶來的壓
力，東協股市 9月僅下跌 2.6%，跌幅仍屬溫和；從
資金面來看 ，外資雖轉為淨賣超 ，但賣超規模明顯
低於第二季調整期間 ，顯示本波修正較偏向漲多後
的獲利了結與資產重新配置 ，而非反映市場對區域
基本面的明顯轉弱或出現恐慌性撤資。
重點摘要
• 東協9月受殖利率與油價上升

非基本面惡化或恐慌性撤資 。

《出口支撐成長敘事未變 ，但高通膨也比預期持久 》
東協及印度經濟仍展現韌性，多數國家8月出口維持
雙位數成長且高於長期平均 ，與全球製造業擴張趨
勢相符 ，其中新加坡及馬來西亞受惠 AI強勁資本支
出，8月出口成長年增率逾 5成，顯示外需仍是重要
成長支撐 。強勁的出口與製造活動不僅有助支撐企
業獲利 ，也在一定程度上抵銷高利率對消費與投資
的壓力 。另一方面 ，印尼、菲律賓等先前高通膨國
家雖已進入降溫趨勢 ，但通膨回落速度仍較緩慢 ，
使央行在降息時程上維持審慎 。整體而言 ，目前東
協與印度景氣尚未出現明顯轉弱 ，但高利率環境可
能維持較市場預期更久 ，意味短期經濟成長動能將
更仰賴外需及製造業活動的支撐。
重點摘要
• 東協及印度出口維持強勁成

長，反映全球製造業景氣仍
具韌性 ，並支撐區域經濟表
現；惟高通膨國家物價降溫
速度偏慢 ，恐使高利率環境
維持更久 ，短期成長仍須仰
賴外需支撐。

《川普表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，重構印度樞紐角色 》
川普近期表態支持印歐經濟走廊 (IMEC)，使這項拜
登任內支持，但因加薩戰爭與區域衝突而停滯近3年
的計畫再度受到市場關注 。隨著中東地緣風險升溫 ，
紅海及荷姆茲海峽等關鍵航道不確定性提高 ，各國
對替代性能源與運輸路徑的需求同步增加 。從規劃
路線來看 ，印歐經濟走廊以印度為起點 ，串連海灣
產油國與歐洲市場 ，若計畫順利推進 ，除有助印度
取得更穩定的中東能源供給 、降低對俄羅斯石油的
依賴外 ，也將進一步鞏固其作為全球製造與供應鏈
樞紐的地位 。對印股而言 ，IMEC有望同時強化能源
安全與供應鏈地位 ，鞏固印度在全球製造的地位 ，
並為基建、工業及製造類股帶來中長期題材支撐。

重點摘要
• 川普表態支持擱置近 3年的印
歐經濟走廊重啟 ，在中東地
緣風險升溫下 ，有助印度強
化能源安全 、降低對俄油依
賴，並鞏固全球製造與供應
鏈樞紐地位。

◼ 貨幣政策與殖利率： 基準情境認為年底前再升
息一碼後，2027上半年進入按兵不動的觀察期 ，
2027年下半年有望轉向寬鬆立場 。推演殖利率
4Q26偏高表現、1H27適度回落。
◼ 投資建議： 股市估值有壓不表示趨勢路徑改變 ，
上半年資金雨露均霑的普漲行情可能逐漸降溫 ，
但獲利能見度高 、基本面持續改善的族群 ，仍
可望獲得資金青睞並展現相對優勢。
熱門議題/資產影響推估 市場焦點
➢ 風險管理式升息而非緊縮週期： 9月FOMC會議全
票通過升息一碼至 3.75%-4.0%，這是自2023年7
月以來的首次升息 。預料2026年底再升息一碼 ，
其後進入觀察期。
➢ 殖利率4Q26偏高表現 、1H27適度回落 ，股市短
線估值有壓 ：美債殖利率通常要等確認升息到頂
後才會回落 ，市場往往在升息前因政策不確定性
而較為震盪；然而 ，一旦後續經濟數據確認景氣
仍處於擴張軌道 ，即使估值面受到利率壓抑 ，企

業獲利成長仍有望成為推動股市續漲的主要動能 。
升息前後的股市震盪不影響趨勢

未見明顯衝擊

聯準會升息落地，股債市未見明顯衝擊
全球投資級債因利率上升而承壓，非投資級債穩健
盤勢回顧
股市情緒受到影響但未失控
3
• 升息預期推高公債及信用債殖利率，但升息落地後有趨穩現象：早在聯準會利率會議召開前夕，市場不斷出現
升息聲浪，推高10年期公債利率一度站上5%，壓抑9月以來信用債市表現，尤其以投資級債表現最為弱勢，也
拖累全年總報酬率轉負 ，但利差走勢其實相當平穩 ，８月以來美投資級債利差大致在 75~85基點區間震盪 。
FOMC會議後公債利率略自高檔回落，並沒有因為升息而持續創高，信用債市殖利率也趨於穩定。
• 股市情緒受到影響但未失控 ：9月以來股市情緒受到一定程度影響 ，不過並未出現恐慌情緒 ，僅利率敏感性類
股震盪較大，非核心消費之外的類股年至今仍多收正報酬。聯準會升息落地後，股市單日跌幅也可控。

©  CTBC
風險管理式升息，而非典型的升息循環重新啟動
2026年底前預期還有一次升息
貨幣政策
通膨廣度其實已有所收斂，預期此一趨勢將延續
4
• 風險管理式升息 ，而非典型的升息循環重新啟動 ：9月FOMC會議全票通過升息一碼至 3.75%-4.0%，這是自
2023年7月以來的首次升息。本次會議的重點可歸納如下：(1)利率預估點陣圖顯示至2026年底前還有一碼的升息
空間；(2)上調通膨與經濟成長預估值，同時下調失業率，是一個很鷹派的組合，不過調整幅度其實很小，2026
年的整體PCE通膨和核心PCE通膨僅上調0.1個百分點；(3)判斷本次會議並非典型的升息循環重新啟動，而是「不
承諾路徑的風險管理式升息」。
• 年底前再升息一碼後進入觀察期 ：搭配利率預估點陣圖，以及本行對2027年通膨走勢的判斷(8月的CPI數據已
經看到通膨廣度已經有收斂態勢，搭配本次升息，預期此一趨勢將延續，2Q27通膨有望降至2.5%以下)，本行
基準情境認為年底前再升息一碼後，2027上半年進入按兵不動的觀察期，2027年下半年有望轉向寬鬆立場。

推演殖利率4Q26偏高表現、1H27適度回落
市場初步反應: 長率未進一步上揚
美債利率
美債殖利率通常要等確認升息到頂後才會回落
5
• 聯準會升息證明公信力，美債市場大致正向表現：9月FOMC會議前市場已先定價升息，而使得美國10年期公債
殖利率來到5%以上，而在FOMC會議後短率上揚、長率大致穩定，顯示市場主要重新定價近期政策變動，而非同
步大幅上修長期通膨或長期利率風險，是一項相對正面的訊號。
• 推演殖利率4Q26偏高表現、1H27適度回落：根據歷史經驗，美債殖利率通常要等確認升息到頂後才會回落，
4Q26在鷹派論調+期中選舉+高油價的影響下，研判美國10年期公債殖利率維持偏高水位 。2027年隨通膨放
緩，市場確認停止升息後，美債殖利率將適度回落。預估未來兩季美國10年期公債殖利率的區間將處於4.5%-
5.1%，美國30年期公債殖利率則在 5.1%-5.5%之間。尾端風險是「後續的升息不斷超過市場預期 」，不過在
油價不持續失控向上的情況下，尾端風險的機率並不高。
資料來源：左， Bloomberg ，2026/9/17，右，3FOURTEEN，2026/9。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/17

過去一個月企業獲利預估值變化
AI成長趨勢
受惠戰爭
• 景氣擴張週期尚未結束 ，升息對股市影響有限 ：9月FOMC決議升息一碼，但股市反應相對有限 。一方面，市
場已提前消化升息預期；更重要的是，過去一年全球景氣維持穩定擴張，且前景仍偏正向。回顧過往景氣擴張
階段的升息循環，市場往往在升息前因政策不確定性而較為震盪；然而 ，一旦後續經濟數據確認景氣仍處於擴
張軌道，即使估值面受到利率壓抑，企業獲利成長仍有望成為推動股市續漲的主要動能。
• 估值壓縮，選股回到獲利能見度：在股市策略方面，現階段獲利成長動能最強的仍是受惠於 AI長期趨勢的科技
供應鏈。部分科技股已於7至8月率先修正整理，不僅評價壓力有所緩解，也提供較具吸引力的布局機會。不過，
中東地緣衝突尚未落幕，加上國際油價維持高檔，使國防、能源等受惠地緣局勢的族群，成為兼具主題性與避
險特性的配置選擇。整體而言，在聯準會重啟升息的環境下，上半年資金雨露均霑的普漲行情可能逐漸降溫，

但獲利能見度高、基本面持續改善的族群，仍可望獲得資金青睞並展現相對優勢。

資料來源：(左) Fed，2026/9/17；(右) ICE BofA indices，2026/9/16 。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/17
• 基本面穩健態勢不變，有助穩定信用債利差：FOMC會議證實美國經濟韌性，在景氣成長及企業獲利正向循環
未結束前，穩健債信將支持信用債利差走勢持穩，雖然目前美國公債利率已大致反應升息預期，但政策不確定
性仍牽動短線較大波動，利率敏感度較低的非投資級債預期將有相對突出的表現 ，而高利率及高債信品質的族
群則可長期持有以息收為主，而外幣債與美國雙政策連動性較低，新當債與澳幣債走勢可望回歸自身貨幣政策。
• 貨幣政策路徑上修帶動美元走強：美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭、油價上漲及通膨預期升溫
支撐，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治與高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。Fed本次升息後美元指
數當日上升約0.6%，美元指數再度回測100。從聯準會官員利率點陣圖判斷，短期利率路徑較先前預期偏高，

支撐美元表現；惟在通膨回穩後 ，貨幣政策仍有機會重新轉向較寬鬆立場 。留意4Q26共和黨失去絕對多數後
對財政樽節的預期，美元短線路徑轉強。美元指數目標價從97上修至99。

## Document 452: ブラジル：右派政権発足の場合、何が変わるのか

Source PDF: all report pdf/ブラジル：右派政権発足の場合、何が変わるのか.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

新興国

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス　〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー

性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和

2026 年 10 月 8 日　全 5 頁

ブラジル：右派政権発足の場合、何が変わる
のか 
市場志向を強めつつ、社会的包摂を維持するフラビオ氏、外交は実
利重視か

経済調査部 シニアエコノミスト 増川 智咲

[要約] 
 2026年10月4日に行われた大統領選挙の第1回投票では、ボルソナロ前大統領の長男
であるフラビオ・ボルソナロ上院議員（以下、フラビオ氏）が首位となったが、過半
数に届く候補者はいなかった。こうした接戦の背景には、現政権の社会福祉政策を評
価する声が根強い一方で、医療や治安、インフレ、低成長などの政策運営に対する不
満も投票行動を左右したとみられる。 
 ルラ政権下では、雇用や実質所得の上昇、信用拡大が消費を押し上げ、貧困削減と内
需主導の成長を支えた。ただし、その成長は資源価格の上昇に伴う交易条件の改善に
も支えられており、生産性向上や持続的な民間投資を通じた成長モデルへの転換は十
分には進まなかった。所得移転による再分配機能を維持しながら、このような構造問
題にも対応することが、次期政権に一段と求められている。 
 フラビオ氏が掲げる2027～30年の政府計画では、1990年代のような「小さな政府」は
追求されておらず、行政組織と歳出の選別、国有資産の民間開放、規制改革を進める
一方、政治的支持の高い社会政策と基礎的公共サービスを維持しようとする構想だ。 
 対外政策では、OECD 加盟やグローバルバリューチェーンへの参加、外資誘致を重視し
ており、少なくとも経済外交の面では、保護主義的な色彩を強める米国とは異なり、
国際経済への統合を重視する姿勢がうかがえる。またそれと並行して、貿易・投資上
の利益を踏まえてグローバルサウスとの関係も維持する、実利的な多角外交を選択す
る可能性が高い。 
 決選投票後の注目点は、右派か左派かの二者択一ではなく、社会的包摂と市場改革と
いった、これまで同国で相反するとされてきた政策をどのように両立していくのかだ
ろう。

### Page 2

1

2 / 5

フラビオ・ボルソナロ氏が首位、ルラ氏との決選投票へ 
2026年10月4日に行われたブラジル大統領選挙の第1回投票では、ボルソナロ前大統領の長
男であるフラビオ・ボルソナロ上院議員（以下、フラビオ氏）が有効票の 47.03％、ルラ現大
統領 1が 45.16％を獲得した。いずれも過半数に届かず、両氏は 10 月 25 日の決選投票に進むこ
ととなった。投票直前の主要世論調査も両氏の接戦を示していたが、実際の投票ではフラビオ
氏が多くの調査予測を上回った。こうした接戦の背景には、ルラ政権の社会福祉政策を評価す
る声が根強い一方で、医療や治安、インフレ、低成長などの政策運営に対する不満も投票行動
を左右したとみられる 2。

ルラ政権：社会政策が支えた内需拡大と残された課題 
ルラ第 1 次・第 2 次政権下では、2003 年に創設された、低所得者層に対する現金給付である
「ボルサ・ファミリア」や、2009 年に導入された「Minha Casa, Minha Vida（私の家、私の人
生） 」といった住宅供給政策を通して、低所得者層を対象とした社会政策を拡充してきた。筆
者が 2010年にブラジルで見学した低所得者向け住宅は、スラム街であるファベーラ住民向けに
供給されたものであった（図表 1） 。簡素な造りで、販売価格は必ずしも低廉ではなかったもの
の、衛生面や治安面ではファベーラを大きく上回り、需要は旺盛であった。また、ショッピン
グモールでは割賦販売が広く利用されており、信用拡大が消費を下支えする構造も見られた
（図表 2） 。 
ルラ政権下では、雇用や実質所得の上昇、信用拡大が消費を押し上げ、貧困削減と内需主導
の成長を支えた。図表 3 は所得階層別の世帯割合を示しているが、貧困層と低所得層とされる
DEクラスの割合が、ルラ第1次政権下の 2003年以降大きく低下し、中間層とされる Cクラスの
割合が大きく拡大したことが分かる。 
ただし、その成長は、資源価格の上昇に伴う交易条件の改善にも支えられており、生産性向
上や持続的な民間投資を通じた成長モデルへの転換は十分には進まなかった。2010 年代半ばに
資源ブームが終焉を迎えると、それまで良好な交易条件の下で覆い隠されてきた低い生産性や
競争力といった構造的な課題が顕在化した。図表 4 は、ブラジルと主要地域・所得国グループ
の労働生産性（労働者一人あたり GDP）を表したものであるが、中所得国全体と比較しても、
ブラジルの労働生産性の低さが目立つ。

1 2003 年から～2010 年まで第 1、2 期を務めた後、2023 年からは第 3 期を務めている。 
2 世論調査機関であるInstituto de Pesquisas Datafolha が 2026 年 5 月に発表した調査によると、現政権に
対する不満には、治安、医療、経済、汚職が挙げられていた。（Datafolha: áreas prioritárias para o 
eleitor são as piores avaliadas no governo Lula 2026 年 5 月 17 日）

ス方式）が 1,175ドル以下、中所得国は 1,176ドル以
上 14,375 ドル、高所得国は 14,376 ドル以上。 
（出所）世界銀行より大和総研作成 
今回の選挙が示しているのは、ルラ政権が 3 期にわたって進めてきた社会福祉政策の意義が
否定されたというよりも、所得移転による再分配機能を維持しながら、これまで十分に解決さ
れてこなかった構造問題にも対応することが、次期政権に一段と求められているということで
あろう。中でも、生産性の向上を通じた競争力の強化はブラジルの持続的な成長に向けた重要
な課題として認識されている。そのためには、規制・制度面の改善や対外開放、効率的な資源
配分、イノベーションの促進等を通じて、国内外から民間投資を呼び込む環境を整備する必要
がある。こうした投資の拡大が生産性の向上とより質の高い雇用の創出につながれば、所得の
0

### Page 4

1

4 / 5

安定を通じて、治安改善を含む社会の安定にも寄与するだろう。したがって、フラビオ政権が
発足した場合にも、政策課題は再分配か成長かの二者択一ではなく、社会的包摂と持続的な成
長をいかに両立させるかにある。

フラビオ氏は市場志向だが、社会的包摂も維持。実利外交の可能性が高い 
図表5は、フラビオ氏が掲げる2027～30年の政府計画である。これらを見ると、省庁の削減
や歳出削減・財政規律、民営化を通じて政府の効率性を高め、民間投資と外国直接投資を呼び
込む方針を示している。また、OECD 加盟、グローバルバリューチェーン 3への参加、競争力の
向上を重視しており、経済運営の軸足はルラ政権より市場志向になる可能性が高い。 
一方、同計画は既存の社会福祉プログラムの維持や、医療・教育面の強化にも言及している。
このため、フラビオ氏の路線を 1990年代の新自由主義的な「小さな政府」とみなすことは適切
ではない。むしろ、行政組織と歳出の選別、国有資産の民間開放、規制改革を進める一方、政
治的支持の厚い社会福祉政策と基礎的公共サービスを維持しようとする構想である。 
特に財政面では、省庁削減や行政改革、歳出の見直しを通じて財政規律を強化する一方、イ
ンフラや治安、医療、教育などの政策課題にも対応する方針を示している。ブラジルでは、政
府部門の貯蓄不足や歳出構造の硬直性が、高金利や民間投資を制約する一因となっており、財
政健全化と成長力強化を両立できるかが重要となる。 
対外政策では、フラビオ氏は親米派とされ、 BRICS を中心とするグローバルサウスとの距離
ができるとの見方もある。しかし、計画を見る限り、国際基準を満たすことによる OECD 加盟、
グローバルバリューチェーンへの参加、外資誘致を重視しており、少なくとも経済外交の面で
は、保護主義的な色彩を強める米国とは異なり、対外開放や国際経済への統合を重視する姿勢
がうかがえる。また、中国との緊密な貿易・投資関係に鑑みると、グローバルサウスと距離を
取ることによる経済的コストを甘受するとも考えにくい。対米関係の強化を図りつつ、貿易・
投資上の利益を踏まえてグローバルサウスとの関係も維持する実利的な多角外交を選択する可
能性が高いだろう。 
以上のように、右派と呼ばれるフラビオ政権が発足した場合も、 「小さな政府」ではなく、
社会福祉政策の維持と市場改革の政策パッケージが取られる可能性が高い。決選投票後の注目
点は、右派か左派かの二者択一ではなく、社会的包摂と市場改革といった、これまで同国で相
反するとされてきた政策をどのように両立していくのか、だろう。

3 製品やサービスが完成するまでの各工程を国際的に分業し、各国が付加価値を生み出しながら連結された生
産ネットワーク。

### Page 5

1

5 / 5

図表 5　フラビオ氏が掲げる 2027～30 年の政府計画

（出所）フラビオ氏政府計画「 Para O Brasil vencer o atraso: Diretrizes do Plano de Governo 2027-
2030」より大和総研作成

政策項目 計画の内容
既存の社会福祉政策の維持 既存の社会福祉プログラムを維持。制度の歪み是正や、不正対策を行う
10省庁以上の削減 政治任用ポストと行政経費の削減

教育 識字率の向上、学校教育の質向上、教育と労働市場のギャップを埋めるため、技術教育の拡充や企業と職業訓練において連携
雇用政策 雇用コストの段階的引下げを通して、正規雇用の拡大（特に18～24歳の初職契約、50歳以上の長期失業者向け契約）、柔軟な勤務体系の実現、求人データベースの整備

## Document 463: 下周，欧洲理事会会议或讨论对华贸易立场，中欧汽车贸易谈判迎核心窗口期

Source PDF: all report pdf/下周，欧洲理事会会议或讨论对华贸易立场，中欧汽车贸易谈判迎核心窗口期.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

10 ⽉ 15-16 ⽇欧洲理事会会议或讨论对华贸易⽴场。由于汽⻋⾏业是本轮谈判中弹性最
⼤、利害最集中的领域，中信证券研究认为此次谈判对汽⻋⾏业的影响最为重⼤，是当下
汽⻋板块最有分歧的⻓期定价因素。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 15-16 ⽇欧洲理事会会议或讨论对华贸易⽴场。由于汽⻋⾏业是本轮谈判中弹性最⼤、利害
最集中的领域，我们认为此次谈判对汽⻋⾏业的影响最为重⼤，是当下汽⻋板块最有分歧的⻓
期定价因素。
今年⻋市内需疲弱，出⼝成为今年很多中国汽⻋企业的利润的核⼼⽀撑，
欧盟是⽬前所有出海市场中盈利能⼒最好的市场
今年以来我国汽⻋市场呈现 “ 内需不振、出⼝强劲 ” 的特征。根据中汽协数据， 2026 年 1-7 ⽉国内
汽⻋批发销量 1146.2 万辆，同⽐下滑 21.4% （其中传统燃料⻋国内销量 -30.1% ）；同期汽⻋整⻋
出⼝ 614 万辆，同⽐增⻓ 66.8% ， 7 ⽉单⽉出⼝ 104.3 万辆、连续两个⽉突破百万辆。按照我们的
统计，去向为欧盟市场的出⼝占总体出⼝⽐例已接近 13% ，出⼝已是今年⻋市为数不多的亮

点。欧盟是除北美以外全球盈利⽔平最⾼的汽⻋市场，我们预期 2026 年为中国⻋企贡献的总净
利润空间约 200-300 亿元。
欧盟对华的汽⻋关税⽬前主要集中在 BEV ，对欧 PHEV 出海过去两年是增⻓
的快⻋道
2024 年 10 ⽉ 31 ⽇起，欧盟开始在 10% 基础关税上，对中国⽣产的 BEV ⻋型加征 7.8%-35.3% 的
反补贴税，为期五年。但该政策执⾏范围并不含 PHEV 和传统燃油⻋。该政策针对不同的企业有
不同的惩罚性税率，普通中国⻋企的惩罚性关税为 20.7% ，此外，特斯拉中国（ 7.8% ）、⽐亚迪
（ 17% ）和吉利汽⻋（ 18.8% ）获得了低于平均⽔平的税率，⽽上汽集团则遭遇了最不公平的
35.3% 的惩罚性关税。纯电受限后，中国⻋企在 2025 年迅速以插混开辟通道： 2025 年中国对欧
PHEV 出⼝同⽐激增 155% ，⽽同期 BEV 对欧出⼝仅增⻓ 12% ； 2026 年⼀季度对欧洲出⼝总量
43.8 万辆（ +84.7% ），其中 BEV 19.8 万辆（ +94.6% ）、 PHEV 10.6 万辆（ +152.4% ）。但今年以

来，有部分欧洲⻋企和政府正在推动对 PHEV ⻋型的贸易壁垒，这也是近⼏个⽉来市场的分歧焦
点。
10 ⽉份是本轮谈判结果出台的核⼼窗⼝期，⻓期规则落地将消除不确定性
我们判断，最终谈判的关税结果存在悲观和乐观两个预期：
（ 1 ）悲观情景：谈判破裂，反补贴税扩围。现⾏ BEV 反补贴税维持不变，并将 7.8%-35.3% 的
加征范围延伸⾄ PHEV ，乃⾄ HEV 领域（最差局⾯）。在此悲观预期下， “ 动⼒迁移套利 ” 通道被
封堵，中国⻋企在欧洲的平均惩罚性关税将升⾄ 21% ，并覆盖全部新能源⻋型，后续只有本⼟
化⽣产才能规避关税。但我们认为，悲观情景发⽣的概率并不⼤。
（ 2 ）乐观情景：中⽅通过价格⾃我约束，获得⼀定的低关税的配额。此⽅案可以参照此前落地
的加拿⼤模式（加⽅给予中国电动⻋每年 4.9 万辆配额、配额内适⽤ 6.1% 关税），欧盟可能会要
求中国对 BEV+PHEV 对欧出⼝实⾏总量配额，对于配额内的⻋型实⾏价格⾃我约束，但在配额
范围内，中⽅⻋型可以享有更低的关税税率。

本地化设⼚的规则也有望在此次谈判后更加明晰

迁，这是中⻓期汽⻋⾏业的投资主线。

## Document 465: 专题研究报告：中外生物医药创新模式研究——基于中美欧日创新生态的比较分析

Source PDF: all report pdf/专题研究报告：中外生物医药创新模式研究——基于中美欧日创新生态的比较分析.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 28
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

专题研究报告
中外生物医药创新模式研究
——基于中美欧日创新生态的比较分析
核心观点 发展研究·专题研究
证券分析师：陶鹂春
0755-81982322
taolc@guosen.com.cn
S0980511040002
相关研究报告
研究缘起与分析界定。生物医药链条长、跨学科，是典型的新兴产业链。中
央层面已把它确定为国家新兴支柱产业，《医药工业发展“十五五”规划》
进一步给出到2030年的预期性指标。本文着重比较承载科学机会的制度，
把“创新模式”界定为从科学选题到价值兑现的闭环——选什么、谁来做、
风险谁担、监管如何接口、价值在哪里兑现。
美国模式：资本驱动的原始创新。领先之处不在资金总量，而在让资金、知
识与退出通道衔接的组织方式：公共部门承担短期内难以商业化的基础研
究，私人资本按里程碑分批下注并保留抽身余地，大药企接过已验证的资产
推向全球。产出上美国长期掌握全球新药首次上市的最高份额，但“首创”
占比有限，高淘汰与融资波动并存。安进与默沙东分别代表“平台研发＋资
本并购”与“重磅产品—专利悬崖—外部再配置”两条路径。

欧洲模式：监管驱动与韧性优先。以统一审评加分散支付守住药品安全底线、
兼顾支付可负担性，把标准、质量与伦理治理做成公共产品；卫生技术评估
的联合评估在推进，支付决策仍留在成员国内，商业回报受碎片化制约。欧
洲的短板不在科学，而在把科学变成收入的能力。罗氏代表组合式创新与规
模现金流再投入型路径，百欧恩泰代表平台突破型路径。
日本模式：大药企内生与稳态转化。让大型药企用内部研发与长期管线消化
不确定性，靠组织能力而非风险资本完成转化。制度底座由专利期限延长、
再审查期与药价调节构成，平台更倾向留在组织内部；产出以连续改良与平
台延展见长，全球领先的原始突破相对有限。控费机制持续左右回报预期：
厚生劳动省已定方向，对销售远超初始预测的少数品种大幅下调药价、上限
较现行水平明显抬高，并纳入下一轮药价制度改革。案例上，武田与第一三
共分别代表内生组织的全球化再造与平台技术的一贯延伸。
中国模式：效率驱动的追赶与突围。以制度改革与工程化能力压缩转化周期：
审评提速带来创新药获批数量快速上升，医保准入节奏前移让新药更快放

量，对外授权成为本土资产接入全球价值链的主要接口，交易数量与金额连
年抬升。短板集中在差异化原始创新的厚度、全球临床证据的说服力与长期
权益的保留能力。案例上，恒瑞医药体现成熟药企的研发再造，百济神州体
现全球商业化突围。
四种模式的比较与全球互嵌。四种模式各有长板，不存在单一最优模式；更
大的变化是创新发生地、标准制定地与商业兑现地开始分离——中国在首次
临床与对外授权上的份额快速上升，最强的商业化与定价能力仍在美国，规
则与标准影响力仍在欧洲。日本与中国也不宜被“东亚模式”一笔带过：前
者是稳态转化模型，后者是效率驱动的供给模型。人工智能作为横向变量同
时作用于四种模式，改变的是转化效率而非制度分野。
2026—2030年展望与结语。这是情景推演而非预测，关键变量有四个：中美
互嵌边界的宽度、支付与创新的平衡、AI的兑现速度、供应链与标准的再分
工。由此对应三种情景：基准情景下合作延续、合规摩擦常态化，中国由供
给中心升级为“供给＋部分全球权益”的合作方；进取情景下证据互认改善，
中国更多参与早期科学发现与全球分成；摩擦情景下审查与数据取用限制扩

### Page 2

据下一轮分工的有利位置。
风险提示：政策不及预期。规则变动、支付收紧。临床与AI转化不及预期。

### Page 5

1.研究缘起与分析界定
本项研究的缘起，首先是对生物医药这条新兴产业链的持续关注。它链条长、跨
学科，从基础研究、临床开发到生产与商业化，各环节由谁承担、如何衔接，各
国差异很大。中央层面已把生物医药确定为国家新兴支柱产业，《医药工业发展“十
五五”规划》进一步给出到2030年的预期性指标。行情与规则动向则提供了近期观
察窗口：9月 21日 A股生物医药指数单日上涨 3.71%、主力资金净流入超 60 亿元；
美国财政部对华投资与授权规则草案倾向放行多数常规授权、限制收窄到病原体
及可武器化技术，但尚未定稿。行情会起伏，更值得长期跟踪的是它背后的结构：
全球药品市场到 2028 年预计达 1.94 万亿美元，总量仍在扩容，但增量背后的创
新来源与价值分配已经改变；未来几年专利到期潮将使品牌药损失约 2,000 亿至
2,360 亿美元，迫使跨国药企持续向外寻找管线。决定结果的往往不是科学机会
本身，而是承载它的制度，这也是本文选择比较四种模式，而非复述行业总量的
原因。
本文所指的“创新模式”是从科学选题到价值兑现的完整闭环：选什么疾病与机

制，由谁承担研发主体，风险由谁分担，监管以什么方式接口，价值最终在哪里
实现。把这五个环节串起来看，同一项科学机会在不同国家和地区会走出完全不
同的路径——在美国常表现为生物技术公司孵化、大型药企并购接棒，在中国则
更可能是先完成本土验证，再靠对外授权或全球临床放大。正因如此，同样的靶
点热度与融资规模，在不同制度环境里对应的风险与回报并不等价。为免用单一
的“成绩单”互相比较，本文讨论产出时会把创新产出、交易产出与商业兑现分
开处理，不把三者混为一谈。
选择美、欧、日、中作为比较对象，不是因为这样凑齐了地域版图，而是因为它
们分别代表一种机制上的典型：美国把高风险探索交给分散的资本市场，用最厚
的支付能力与最活跃的并购市场为失败定价；欧洲以统一审评加分散支付守住安
全与可及，把标准、质量与伦理治理做成公共产品；日本让大型药企用内部研发
和长期管线消化不确定性，靠组织能力而不是风险资本完成转化；中国则用制度
改革与工程化能力压缩转化周期，以效率换取增量供给。近十年最明显的变化，
是创新的发生地与商业兑现地开始分离——中国在首次临床和对外授权上的份额

快速上升，最大的商业化与定价能力却仍在美国，四者因此更像分工关系，而不
是同一条赛道上的快慢之分。任何“谁该模仿谁”的处方，最后都会撞上各自的
制度边界。
本报告先把四种模式各自的制度底座、运行闭环与产出边界讲清楚，再把它们放
进同一张全球网络里看分工与各自的份量，最后才谈未来五年可能出现的变化。
这是因为四种模式的差异只有在相互嵌入的关系里才能被正确理解。人工智能则
被当作一个横向变量处理——它同时作用于四种模式，改变的是从投入到可验证
资产的转化效率，而不是制度本身的分野；把它放到比较之后单独讨论，是为了
避免把技术热度误读成模式差异的消失。
2.美国模式：资本驱动的原始创新
在美国生物医药领域，领先并不能简单归结为“钱多”。真正稀缺的不是资金总
量，而是一套让资金、知识与退出通道相互衔接的组织方式：公共部门积累暂时
无法变现的科学，私人资本按里程碑分批下注并随时可以抽身，大药企接过已获

### Page 6

验证的资产再推向全球市场。
2.1.模式素描与制度底座：公共知识打底、分散资本接力
美国模式的制度构造由几个互相咬合的部件组成：公共科研供给暂时无法变现的
科学机会，能进能退的私人资本按里程碑分担开发风险，知识产权与人员流动把
发现整理成可估值资产，市场与支付方在末端圈定回报上限；抽掉任何一个部件，
循环都会卡住。
知识底座靠联邦科研的长期投入。美国国家科学基金会下设的国家科学与工程统
计中心（NCSES）数据显示，2023 年全美研发执行约 9,400 亿美元，企业执行 7,350
亿美元、占 78%，高校 1,020亿美元、占 11%，联邦政府自身 740亿美元、占 8%；
按经费来源，企业出钱约占 75%、联邦约 18%。
图1：2023年美国研发执行结构（按执行部门）
资料来源：美国国家科学基金会NCSES，国信证券经济研究所整理。
但企业占比高不等于联邦政府可以退场，公共资金最不可替代的作用是知识外溢。
2025 年《Science》一项研究挑出 1980—2007年间 NIH 资助排序中位列后 40%的

项目，回溯 2000—2023 年 FDA 批准的 557 个小分子新药：40 个（7.1%）专利明
示受 NIH 资助，286 个（51.4%）至少引用过一项后 40%的成果，65 个（11.7%）
超过四分之一引文落在该类研究上——这只能说明外溢面很广，不能当作“半数
新药由 NIH制造”的证据。
表1：2000—2023年 FDA批准小分子新药与 NIH资助研究的引用关联
指标 数量或比例 解释边界
2000—2023 年 FDA 批准小分子新药 557 项 仅为论文所限的小分子药样本
专利直接承认 NIH 资助 40 项（7.1%） “直接”不等于剔除公共资金后药物无法开发
专利引用后 40% NIH 资助研究 286 项（51.4%） 引文关系反映知识联系，不等于因果贡献
至少 25%引文指向后 40%项目 65 项（11.7%） 降低偶然引用解释，但仍非排他性因果识别
资料来源：Azoulay P, Clancy M, Li D, Sampat BN. "What if NIH had been 40% smaller?" Science, 2025。
